In the rest of this section we will see that the theory of principal Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-spaces, studied in the previous section, extends to the toric setting. In particular, in section
4.1
, we extend the correspondence between principal Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-spaces and Lagrangian fibrations by showing that toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-spaces are essentially the same thing as toric Lagrangian fibrations, i.e., singular Lagrangian fibrations with only elliptic singularities.

4.1.
Toric Lagrangian fibrations

We consider the following special class of singular Lagrangian fibrations.

Definition 4.2
.

By a
singular Lagrangian fibration
we mean a symplectic manifold
(S,\omega)
together with a smooth map
\mu:S\to M
satisfying the following properties:

(i)

\dim(S)=2\dim(M)
;

(ii)

\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
is a Poisson map, where
M
is equipped with the zero Poisson structure;

(iii)

\mu
has connected fibers, is proper as a map onto its image and its regular points form a dense open set in
S
.

A
toric Lagrangian fibration
is a singular Lagrangian fibration whose only singularities are of elliptic type.

An
elliptic type
singularity is the simplest non-degenerate singularity that a singular Lagrangian fibration can possess – see, e.g.,
[
18
,
37
]
. Instead of giving its definition, we recall the following characterization.

Theorem 4.3

(Dufour and Molino
[
18
]
)
.

Let
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
be a singular Lagrangian fibration. A singular point
p_{0}\in S
is elliptic if and only if there is a neighborhood
U
of
x_{0}:=\mu(p_{0})
and an embedding
\phi:U\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
, such that
\phi\circ\mu:(\mu^{-1}(U),\omega)\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the moment map of an effective
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-action.

Note that the
regular fibers
of a singular Lagrangian fibration are indeed Lagrangian submanifolds. For a general singular Lagrangian fibration a fiber of
\mu:S\to M
can contain both regular and singular points. However, the previous result implies that for a
toric
Lagrangian fibration
\mu:S\to M
this cannot happen: the rank of
\mathrm{d}\mu
is the same for all points in the same fiber. Hence, for a toric fibration the set of regular points is a dense open set
\mathring{S}\subset S
saturated by
\mu
-fibers. It follows that
\mathring{\Delta}:=\mu(\mathring{S})
is also an open subset of
M
which is dense in the image
\Delta:=\mu(S)
. Therefore, the restriction
\mu:(\mathring{S},\omega)\to\mathring{\Delta}
is a (regular) Lagrangian fibration so, as we saw in Section
3.1
, there is an induced integral affine structure
\mathring{\Lambda}
on
\mathring{\Delta}
and
\mu:(\mathring{S},\omega)\to\mathring{\Delta}
is a principal Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}
-space.

The following result shows that the integral affine structure on
\mathring{\Delta}
extends uniquely to an open
M^{\prime}\subset M
containing
\Delta
, so
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M^{\prime}
becomes a toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-space.

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
be a toric Lagrangian fibration. Then there is an open
M^{\prime}\subset M
containing

\mu(S)
and an integral affine structure
\Lambda
on
M^{\prime}
such that
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M^{\prime}
is a toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}
-space. The germ of
(M^{\prime},\Lambda)
is unique. Conversely, every toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-space is a toric Lagrangian fibration.

Proof.

It follows from Theorem
4.3
that
\Delta:=\mu(S)
is a codimension 0 submanifold with corners of
M
, with interior
\mathring{\Delta}=\mu(\mathring{S})
. Moreover, the integral affine structure
\mathring{\Lambda}
on
\mathring{\Delta}
extends to the boundary
\partial\Delta
, so
\Delta
is an integral affine manifold with corners. In fact, around a boundary point
x_{0}\in\partial\Delta
an integral affine chart is provided by the restriction
\phi:V\cap\Delta\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
of the embedding given by Theorem
4.3
.

Recall that any manifold with corners embeds as a codimension 0 submanifold of a manifold without corners and the germ of such embeddings is unique. Similarly, for embeddings of integral affine manifolds with corners we have the following lemma, which proves the first part of the statement of the theorem.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
(\Delta,\Lambda)
be an integral affine manifold manifold with corners.

(i)

Given any a codimension 0 embedding of
\Delta
in a manifold without corners
M
, the integral affine structure
\Lambda
extends to an open neighborhood of
\Delta
in
M
.

(ii)

The germ of codimension 0 integral affine embeddings of
(\Delta,\Lambda)
in integral affine manifolds without corners is unique.

Proof of the Lemma
4.5
.

Any integral affine manifold manifold with corners can be embedded as a codimension 0 integral affine submanifold of an integral affine manifold manifold without corners. The proof is entirely similar to the proof given in
[
35
]
that any real analytic manifold admits a complexification (see also
[
17
]
). Fix such an embedding
\phi:(\Delta,\Lambda)\to(N,\Lambda_{N})
and let
\psi:\Delta\to M
be any codimension 0 embedding in a manifold without corners. Using Whitney’s embedding theorem we can assume that
N\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
and, by a partition of unity argument, that there is a smooth map
F:W\to\mathbb{R}^{p}
extending the identity on
\Delta
, defined on some open neighborhood of
\Delta
in
M
. Choosing a retraction
r:U\to N
of some neighborhood of
N
in
\mathbb{R}^{p}
, the composition
r\circ F:F^{-1}(U)\to U
restricts to the identity on
\Delta
. Hence, possibly after shrinking
W
, we obtain a local diffeomorphism
r\circ F:W\to N
, that extends the identity on
\Delta
. Pulling back
\Lambda_{N}
by this diffeomorphism, we obtain an extension of
\Lambda
from
\Delta
to an integral affine structure on the neighborhood
W
. This proves item (i).

Item (ii) in the lemma follows from item (i), since two integral affine structures on a manifold
M
which agree on
\Delta
they must agree on some open neighborhood of
\Delta
, because
\Delta
has codimension 0.
∎

It remains to prove the last statement in the theorem. Given a toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-space
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
and a point
p_{0}\in S
, fixing a integral affine chart
(U,\phi)
around
x_{0}:=\mu(p_{0})
we have that
\phi\circ\mu:(\mu^{-1}(U),\omega)\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the moment map of an effective
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-action. If this action is free,
p_{0}
is a regular point. Otherwise,
p_{0}
is a singular point which, by Theorem
4.3
, is elliptic. Hence,
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
is a toric Lagrangian fibration.
∎

It follows from the previous results that the standard local model for toric symplectic manifolds is also a local model for toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-spaces. Let
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
be a toric
(\mathcal{T},\Omega)
-space with
\Delta:=\mu(S)
and let
x\in\Delta
. As we saw before, a choice of integral affine chart
(U,\phi=(x^{1},\dots,x^{n}))
centered at
x
gives a trivialization of the symplectic torus bundle

\Phi:(\mathcal{T},\Omega)|_{U}\simeq(\mathbb{T}^{n}\times U,\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}\theta_{i}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{i}).

Via this local isomorphism the toric
\mathcal{T}
-action corresponds to a toric
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-action with moment map

\phi\circ\mu:(\mu^{-1}(U),\omega)\to\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Since
\mu
has connected fibers and is proper onto its image, it follows from the standard normal form for toric manifolds (see, e.g.,
[
26
, Lemma B.3]
) that, around
x
, there is a local isomorphism between this Hamiltonian
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-space and the standard local model. The latter is the Hamiltonian
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-space consisting of:

•

the symplectic manifold

\left(S_{k,n},\omega_{k,n}\right):=(\mathbb{C}^{k}\times\mathbb{T}^{n-k}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k},\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\mathrm{d}z_{j}\wedge\mathrm{d}\overline{z}_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-k}\mathrm{d}\theta_{j}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{j});

•

the moment map is given by

\mu_{k,n}:\mathbb{C}^{k}\times\mathbb{T}^{n-k}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}\to\mathbb{R}^{n},\quad(z,\theta,x)\mapsto(|z_{1}|^{2},\dots,|z_{k}|^{2},x),

with image
\Delta=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{k}
;

•

the
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-action given by

(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\cdot(z,\theta,x):=(e^{2\pi i\tau_{1}}z,\tau_{2}+\theta,x),\quad(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\in\mathbb{T}^{k}\times\mathbb{T}^{n-k}=\mathbb{T}^{n}.

(4.1)

So, after possibly changing the integral affine chart
\phi
, we obtain an isomorphism
(\phi,\Phi,\Psi)
from the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}|_{U}
-space
\mu:(\mu^{-1}(U),\omega)\to U
to the Hamiltonian
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-space
\mu:(\mu_{k,n}^{-1}(V),\omega_{k,n})\to V
, where
V:=\phi(U)\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Henceforth, we will refer to such an isomorphism, or to
(z,\theta,x)
, as
standard toric coordinates
for
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
around
x
.

For future reference, we state the following result concerning invariant functions on the local model.

Proposition 4.6
.

Consider
S:=\mathbb{C}^{k}\times\mathbb{T}^{n-k}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k}
, equipped with the
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-action (
4.1
) and let
U
be an open in
\Delta:=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{k}
. For any
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant smooth function
f
on
\mu^{-1}(U)
, the following hold:

(a)

The unique function
\underline{f}
on
U
such that
f=\mu^{*}\underline{f}
is smooth, i.e., it is the restriction of a smooth function defined on some open in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
;

(b)

If
f
vanishes on
\mu^{-1}(U)\cap\{z_{j}=0\}
for some
j\in\{1,\dots,k\}
, then it is of the form
f=|z_{j}|^{2}\cdot\widehat{f}
for a unique
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant smooth function
\widehat{f}
on
\mu^{-1}(U)
.

Proof.

Part (a) is well-known: it readily follows from Schwartz’ theorem. Part (b) is immediate from part (a) and the fact that if
h\in C^{\infty}(U)
vanishes on
U\cap\{x^{j}=0\}
, then
h=x^{j}\widehat{h}
, for some
\widehat{h}\in C^{\infty}(U)
.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

In the literature singular Lagrangian fibrations admitting local effective
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-actions have been study by several authors
[
6
,
36
,
37
,
38
]
. The result above shows that this form of symmetry can be encoded globally by toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-actions. Moreover, the results to follow will show that this approach gives an effective, coordinate free way of studying the geometry of such fibrations.

4.2.
Classification of toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-spaces

Delzant’s classification of compact symplectic toric manifolds in terms of polytopes (
[
15
]
) was extended to toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-spaces in
[
28
]
. In this more general setup, Delzant’s classification and Duistermaat’s classification of Lagrangian fibrations (cf. Theorem
3.4
) are combined, as we will now explain.

In Delzant’s classification, the polytope corresponding to a symplectic toric manifold is the image of the moment map and lies in the integral affine manifold
(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbb{Z}^{n})
. These Delzant polytopes satisfy certain integrality conditions. For toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-spaces, the image of
\mu
is a more general object called a
Delzant subspace
of the integral affine manifold
(M,\Lambda)
, the base of the symplectic torus bundle
(\mathcal{T},\Omega)
.

Definition 4.8
.

A
Delzant subspace

\Delta
of an
m
-dimensional integral affine manifold
(M,\Lambda)
is a subspace of
M
with the property that for each
x\in\Delta
there is an integral affine chart
(U,\phi)
for
(M,\Lambda)
centered at
x
such that
\phi(U\cap\Delta)
is an open in
\mathbb{R}^{m}_{k}:=[0,\infty[^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k}
for some
k\in\{0,\dots,m\}
(depending on
x
).

In other words, a Delzant subspace of
(M,\Lambda)
is a codimension zero integral affine submanifold with corners.

Example 4.9

(Compact symplectic toric manifolds)
.

For any compact symplectic toric manifold
\mu_{0}:(S,\omega)\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the image of the moment map is a Delzant polytope in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Delzant polytopes are precisely the compact and connected Delzant subspaces of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
equipped with the standard integral affine structure.

Example 4.10

(Non-compact symplectic toric manifolds)
.

Non-compact symplectic toric manifold
\mu_{0}:(S,\omega)\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
also fit into our framework. It was observed in
[
26
]
that the orbit space
S/\mathbb{T}^{n}
is a manifold with corners and the induced map

\phi:S/\mathbb{T}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}

is a local embedding. The pullback along
\phi
of the standard integral affine structure in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
makes
\Delta:=S/\mathbb{T}^{n}
an integral affine manifold with corners. By Lemma
4.5
,
\Delta
embeds as a Delzant subspace of an integral affine manifold
(M,\Lambda)
without corners. Note that the composition of the quotient map with this embedding

\mu:S\to\Delta\hookrightarrow M,

yields a toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}
-space
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
.

Example 4.11
.

The Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-spaces in examples
2.20
and
2.21
are toric and the image of their moment maps are Delzant spaces. In fact:

•

For the standard integral affine cylinder
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}
, appearing in Example
2.20
,
\Delta=[a,b]\times\mathbb{S}^{1}
is a Delzant subspace, for any real numbers
a<b
.

•

Similarly, for the exotic integral affine cylinder
]-2,\infty[\times\mathbb{S}^{1}
of Example
2.21
,
\Delta=[a,b]\times\mathbb{S}^{1}
is also a Delzant subspace, for any
-2<a<b
.

A version of the classification theorem for toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-spaces can be stated as follows. For a complete proof and details we refer to
[
28
]
.

Theorem 4.12

(
[
28
]
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{T},\Omega)\to(M,\Lambda)
be a symplectic torus bundle. For any toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-space
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
, the image
\Delta:=\mu(S)
is a Delzant subspace of
(M,\Lambda)
. Moreover, such Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-spaces are classified by their:

(i)

Delzant subspace
\Delta:=\mu(S)
, and

(ii)

Lagrangian-Chern class
c_{1}(S,\omega)\in\check{H}^{1}(\Delta,\underline{\mathcal{T}_{\textrm{Lag}}})
.

Here
\underline{\mathcal{T}_{\textrm{Lag}}}
denotes the sheaf of Lagrangian sections of
\mathcal{T}|_{\Delta}
and the Lagrangian-Chern class is defined similarly to the regular case (see
[
28
]
) and the proof of Theorem
4.31
).

Remark 4.13
.

As for classical toric symplectic manifolds
[
15
]
, the map

\underline{\mu}:S/\mathcal{T}\to M

(4.2)

is a homeomorphism onto
\Delta:=\mu(S)
. Moreover, it respects the natural stratifications on
S/\mathcal{T}
and
\Delta
, namely:

•

\Delta
is naturally stratified, being a manifold with corners;

•

S/\mathcal{T}
is naturally stratified, being the orbit space of a proper Lie groupoid action
[
31
]
.

We refer to
[
28
]
for details.

Remark 4.14
.

The classification result above, beside generalizing Duistermaat and Delzant classification results, is also closely related to classification results in various other settings, as in Remark
4.7
. For example, the classification of non-compact symplectic toric manifolds due to Karshon and Lerman
[
26
]
follows from this theorem.

4.3.
Classical symplectic toric manifolds

Motivated by Example
4.10
we introduce the following definition.

Definition 4.15
.

A toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-space
\mu:(S^{2n},\omega)\to M
is called a
classical symplectic toric manifold
if there exists an effective Hamiltonian
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-action on
(S,\omega)
with moment map
\mu_{0}:S\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
and a local embedding
\phi:\Delta\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\mu_{0}=\phi\circ\mu.

The toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-spaces that are classical symplectic toric manifolds can be easily characterized in terms of the integral affine geometry of their Delzant subspaces.

Theorem 4.16
.

A Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-space
\mu:(S^{2n},\omega)\to M
is a classical symplectic toric manifold if and only if the associated Delzant space
\Delta=\mu(S)
has trivial affine holonomy.

Proof.

The Delzant space
\Delta=\mu(S)
has trivial affine holonomy if and only if the developing map
\operatorname{dev}_{x_{0}}:\widetilde{\Delta}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
factors through the covering map to an integral affine local embedding
\phi:\Delta\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

On the other hand, a map
\mu_{0}:S\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
which factors through a local embedding
\phi:\Delta\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\mu_{0}=\phi\circ\mu,

is the moment map of an effective Hamiltonian
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-action if and only if
\phi
is integral affine. Hence, the result follows.
∎

Recall that a compact toric symplectic manifold is always 1-connected. In this respect we also have the following result.

Corollary 4.17
.

A Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-space
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
whose associated Delzant space
\Delta=\mu(S)
is 1-connected is a classical symplectic toric manifold. In particular this holds whenever
S
is 1-connected.

Proof.

The first part of the statement follows immediately from the theorem. For the second part, consider the inclusions

\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{\Delta}
\textstyle{\mathring{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu|_{\mathring{S}}}
\textstyle{\mathring{\Delta}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The map
\mu:\mathring{S}\to\Delta
is a principal
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-bundle while
\mathring{\Delta}
and
\Delta
have the same homotopy type. Therefore, passing to fundamental groups we obtain a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\pi_{1}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{*}}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(\Delta)}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(\mathring{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(\mathring{\Delta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

where the bottom horizontal arrow is surjective. It follows that
\mu_{*}:\pi_{1}(S)\to\pi_{1}(\Delta)
is also surjective. Hence, if
S
is 1-connected, so is
\Delta
.
∎

Corollary 4.18
.

A Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-space
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
with
S
compact is a classical symplectic toric manifold if and only if
S
is 1-connected.

Note that non-compact symplectic toric manifolds may fail to be 1-connected (e.g.,
S=T^{*}\mathbb{T}^{n}
). Hence, the last corollary does not hold if one removes the compactness assumption.

The elliptic and
b
-tangent bundle

-tangent bundle

-tangent bundle

– with smooth local frames given by (

). Here

dual to (

) consists of those smooth sections that restrict to the
1
-forms

\frac{1}{x^{1}}\mathrm{d}x^{1},\dots,\frac{1}{x^{k}}\mathrm{d}x^{k},\mathrm{d}x^{k+1},\dots,\mathrm{d}x^{n},

on
\mathring{\Delta}
. In summary, smooth sections of the

-cotangent bundle can be represented by smooth
1
-forms on

with poles of order at most

at the boundary.

Next, we turn to residues. The open faces of
\Delta
are the leaves of the Lie algebroid
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-9.24052pt{b}\kern 7.62733pt}}_{{\kern-12.54509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.62733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-7.06128pt{b}\kern 5.44809pt}}_{{\kern-10.36584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.44809pt}}}
. Accordingly, for each open face
F
of
\Delta
we have a short exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ker(\rho)|_{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-17.53314pt{b}\kern 15.31912pt}}_{{\kern-22.15953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.31912pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-17.53314pt{b}\kern 15.31912pt}}_{{\kern-22.15953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.31912pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-11.49052pt{b}\kern 9.87733pt}}_{{\kern-14.79509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 9.87733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-9.31128pt{b}\kern 7.69809pt}}_{{\kern-12.61584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.69809pt}}}|_{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{TF\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

When
F
is a facet, i.e., a face of codimension
1
in
\Delta
,
\ker(\rho)|_{F}
is a line bundle that admits a canonical non-vanishing section, which can be characterized as follows.

Proposition 4.19
.

Let
F
be an open facet of a manifold with corners
\Delta
. There is a unique non-vanishing section

\mathcal{E}_{F}\in\Gamma^{\infty}(\ker(\rho)|_{F})

with the property that any
X\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-8.11684pt{b}\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-12.74323pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-8.11684pt{b}\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-12.74323pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-5.2882pt{b}\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-8.59276pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-4.23819pt{b}\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-7.54276pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt}}}(U)
that is Euler-like with respect to
F
extends
\mathcal{E}_{F}|_{U\cap F}
.

Here by an
Euler-like vector field

X\in\mathfrak{X}(\Delta)
relative to the submanifold
F\subset\Delta
we mean a vector field such that
X|_{F}=0
and the linearization of
X
along
F
induces the identity map on the normal bundle to
F
in
\Delta
. This proposition is well-known (see, e.g.,
[
9
,
25
]
) so we omit the proof.

Remark 4.20
.

Let
(U,x^{1},\dots,x^{n})
be coordinates for
\Delta
onto an open in
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{k}
. Writing a given
b
-vector field
X\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-8.11684pt{b}\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-12.74323pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-8.11684pt{b}\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-12.74323pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-5.2882pt{b}\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-8.59276pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-4.23819pt{b}\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-7.54276pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt}}}(U)
as

X=\sum_{i=1}^{k}f_{i}\,x^{i}\,{\partial}_{x^{i}}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{n}f_{i}\,{\partial}_{x^{i}},\quad f_{i}\in C^{\infty}_{\Delta}(U),

then
X
is Euler-like with respect to
F
if and only if for each
x\in U\cap F

f_{i}(x)=\begin{cases}1\quad&\textrm{ if }x^{i}=0\text{ and }i\leq k,\\
\\
0\quad&\textrm{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

In particular, if
k=1
, then
x^{1}\partial_{x^{1}}\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-8.11684pt{b}\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-12.74323pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-8.11684pt{b}\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-12.74323pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-5.2882pt{b}\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-8.59276pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-4.23819pt{b}\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-7.54276pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt}}}(U)
is an extension of
\mathcal{E}_{F}|_{U\cap F}
.

Using the previous proposition the residue of a vector-valued 1-form can be defined as follows.

Definition 4.21
.

Let
\Delta
be a manifold with corners and
F
an open facet of
\Delta
. The
residue along
F
of a vector-valued
1
-form
\alpha\in\Omega^{1}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-9.24052pt{b}\kern 7.62733pt}}_{{\kern-12.54509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.62733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-7.06128pt{b}\kern 5.44809pt}}_{{\kern-10.36584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.44809pt}}},E)
is the section

\mathrm{res}_{F}(\alpha):=\alpha(\mathcal{E}_{F})\in\Gamma^{\infty}(E|_{F}).

4.4.2.
Background on the elliptic tangent bundle

The elliptic tangent bundle
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}}
of a toric
(\mathcal{T},\Omega)
-space
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
is a Lie algebroid over
S
, similar to the
b
-tangent bundle introduced in the previous subsection. This Lie algebroid was first introduced in
[
9
]
in the case of a single elliptic divisor and then generalized for several divisors, including the case of a classical symplectic toric action, in
[
10
,
11
]
. In our case, we can define the elliptic tangent bundle more directly in terms of its sheaf of sections as follows.

Recall that
\Delta:=\mu(S)
is a submanifold with corners of
M
, so that it comes with a natural sheaf of smooth functions
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{\Delta}
. Consider the sheaf
\mu_{*}(\mathfrak{X}_{S})^{\mathcal{T}}
on
\Delta
that assigns to an open
V
the vector space of
\mathcal{T}
-invariant vector fields on the invariant open
\mu^{-1}(V)
in
S
. This is naturally a sheaf of
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{\Delta}
-modules, which is locally free of rank
2n
, where
n=\dim(\Delta)=\frac{1}{2}\dim(S)
. Indeed, in the standard toric coordinates
(u_{j}+iv_{j},\theta_{l},x^{l})
around a point
x\in\Delta
of depth
k
– see Proposition
4.6
– a basis for
\mu_{*}(\mathfrak{X}_{S})^{\mathcal{T}}
around
x
is given by the vector fields

\partial_{\phi_{1}},\dots,\partial_{\phi_{k}},r_{1}\partial_{r_{1}},\dots,r_{k}\partial_{r_{k}},\dots,\partial_{\theta_{1}},\dots,\partial_{\theta_{n-k}},\partial_{x^{1}},\dots,\partial_{x^{n-k}}.

(4.5)

Here our convention for the angular and radial vector fields on
\mathbb{C}^{k}
is so that

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\partial_{\phi_{j}}

\displaystyle=u_{j}\partial_{v_{j}}-v_{j}\partial_{u_{j}},

\displaystyle r_{j}\partial_{r_{j}}

\displaystyle=u_{j}\partial_{u_{j}}+v_{j}\partial_{v_{j}}.

The sheaf of
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{\Delta}
-modules
\mu_{*}(\mathfrak{X}_{S})^{\mathcal{T}}
pulls back along
\mu
to a sheaf
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-8.96642pt{\mu}\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-15.46188pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-8.96642pt{\mu}\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-15.46188pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-5.89503pt{\mu}\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-10.53465pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-4.84503pt{\mu}\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-9.48465pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt}}}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{S}
-modules, which is locally free of the same rank. More concretely,
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-8.96642pt{\mu}\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-15.46188pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-8.96642pt{\mu}\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-15.46188pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-5.89503pt{\mu}\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-10.53465pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-4.84503pt{\mu}\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-9.48465pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt}}}
can be viewed as the subsheaf of
\mathfrak{X}_{S}
consisting of those vector fields on
S
locally generated by
\mathcal{T}
-invariant vector fields. We will call
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-8.96642pt{\mu}\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-15.46188pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-8.96642pt{\mu}\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-15.46188pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-5.89503pt{\mu}\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-10.53465pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathfrak{X}^{{\kern-4.84503pt{\mu}\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-9.48465pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt}}}
the
sheaf of elliptic vector fields
on
S
relative to
\mu
. The
elliptic tangent bundle

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}}
is the corresponding vector bundle over
S
. Like the
b
-tangent bundle,
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}}
has a natural Lie algebroid structure. The anchor is the canonical map

which identifies its sheaf of sections with

, and the Lie bracket is the usual Lie bracket of vector fields. By construction, the vector fields (

. The elliptic tangent bundle allows to resolve the singularities of the differential of
\mu
and of the infinitesimal action

(the ranks of which drop at points outside of

), in the following sense.

Proposition 4.22
.

The differential of
\mu
lifts uniquely to a fiberwise surjective Lie algebroid map

\textstyle{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-9.24052pt{b}\kern 7.62733pt}}_{{\kern-12.54509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.62733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-7.06128pt{b}\kern 5.44809pt}}_{{\kern-10.36584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.44809pt}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{TS\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}\mu}
\textstyle{T\Delta}

Moreover, the infinitesimal action lifts uniquely to a vector bundle map
\mathbscr{a}_{*}

\textstyle{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mu^{*}(T^{*}M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbscr{a}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\text{ }\text{ }\text{ }\mathbscr{a}}
\textstyle{TS}

that is fiberwise injective and has image
\ker(\mu_{*})
.

Proof.

The first statement follows from the remark that any invariant vector field on
S
is
\mu
-related to a unique
b
-vector field on
\Delta
, which is readily verified using the bases (
4.5
). The second statement follows from the fact that
\mathbscr{a}(\alpha)
is an invariant vector field tangent to
\ker(\mathrm{d}\mu)
for every
1
-form
\alpha
.
∎

Finally, we recall the notion of radial residue. As for classical symplectic toric manifolds, for a toric
(\mathcal{T},\Omega)
-space
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
the stratification of
S
induced by the
\mathcal{T}
-action consists of the submanifolds of
S
of the form
S_{F}:=\mu^{-1}(F)
, with
F
an open face of
\Delta:=\mu(S)
. These strata are the leaves of the Lie algebroid
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}}
. In particular, for any open face
F
of
\Delta
, we have a short exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ker(\rho)|_{S_{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}}|_{S_{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{TS_{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

If
F
is an open facet of
\Delta
, then
\ker(\rho)|_{S_{F}}
is a rank
2
vector bundle that admits a canonical non-vanishing section, which can characterized as follows.

Proposition 4.23
.

Let
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
be a toric
(\mathcal{T},\Omega)
-space and let
F
be an open facet of
\Delta:=\mu(S)
. There is a unique non-vanishing section

\mathcal{E}_{S_{F}}\in\Gamma^{\infty}(\ker(\rho)|_{S_{F}})

such that any
\mathcal{T}
-invariant vector field on an invariant open
U
in
S
that is Euler-like with respect to
S_{F}
extends
\mathcal{E}_{S_{F}}|_{U\cap S_{F}}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{E}_{S_{F}}
is
\mu_{*}
-related to
2\mathcal{E}_{F}
.

Remark 4.24
.

In standard toric coordinates, as in (
4.5
), a
\mathcal{T}
-invariant vector field
X
on
U
can be written as

X=\sum_{i=1}^{k}f^{r}_{i}r_{i}\partial_{r_{i}}+f^{\phi}_{i}\partial_{\phi_{i}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-k}f^{\theta}_{j}\partial_{\theta_{j}}+f^{x}_{j}{\partial}_{x^{j}},

for some invariant functions
f^{r}_{i},f^{\phi}_{i},f^{\theta}_{j},f^{x}_{j}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)^{\mathcal{T}}
. Then
X
is Euler-like with respect to
S_{F}
if and only if for each
p=(z,t,x)\in U\cap S_{F}
one has

f_{i}^{\phi}(p)=0,\quad f_{j}^{\theta}(p)=0,\quad f_{j}^{x}(p)=0\quad\text{and}\quad f_{i}^{r}(p)=\begin{cases}1\quad&\textrm{ if }z_{i}=0,\\
0\quad&\textrm{ otherwise},\end{cases}

for all
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
and
j\in\{1,\dots,n-k\}
. In particular, if
k=1
, then
r\partial_{r}
is an extension of
\mathcal{E}_{S_{F}}|_{U\cap S_{F}}
.

Similar to the definition of residues for vector-valued
b
-forms, we can define residues of vector-valued elliptic 1-forms as follows.

Definition 4.25
.

Let
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
be a toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-space,
F
an open facet of
\Delta:=\mu(S)
and
E
a vector bundle over
\Delta
. The
radial residue along
F
of a vector-valued
1
-form
\alpha\in\Omega^{1}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}},E)
is the section

\mathrm{res}_{F,\mathrm{Rad}}(\alpha):=\alpha(\mathcal{E}_{S_{F}})\in\Gamma^{\infty}(E|_{S_{F}}).

4.5.
Elliptic Lagrangian connections

We will now show that Theorem
3.15
extends to toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-spaces. For that, we need an appropriate notion of connection
1
-form on a toric
\mathcal{T}
-space
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
. First, notice that there is a canonical fiberwise linear
\mathcal{T}
-action on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}}
along the composition of the bundle projection with
\mu
, because
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}}
is the pull-back along
\mu
of a vector bundle on
\Delta:=\mu(S)
. For any
p\in S
the action by an element

is the canonical isomorphism
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T_{p}S^{{\kern-17.12164pt{\mu}\kern 14.05804pt}}_{{\kern-23.61711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 14.05804pt}}_{{\kern-23.61711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.14026pt}}_{{\kern-13.99991pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern p}S^{{\kern-20.9957pt{\mu}\kern p}S^{{\kern-14.07921pt{\mu}\kern p}S^{{\kern-12.12746pt{\mu}\kern 9.90742pt}}}
. The anchor map of the elliptic tangent bundle is
\mathcal{T}
-equivariant with respect to this action and the tangent action of

.

Definition 4.26
.

An
elliptic connection
1
-form
for a toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-space
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
is a Lie algebroid form
\theta\in\Omega^{1}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}},T^{*}M)
which satisfies:

(i)

\mathcal{T}
-invariance:
\theta_{gp}(g\cdot v)=\theta_{p}(v)
, for all
v\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T_{p}S^{{\kern-17.12164pt{\mu}\kern 14.05804pt}}_{{\kern-23.61711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 14.05804pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T_{p}S^{{\kern-17.12164pt{\mu}\kern 14.05804pt}}_{{\kern-23.61711pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 14.05804pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T_{p}S^{{\kern-11.31204pt{\mu}\kern 9.09201pt}}_{{\kern-15.95166pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 9.09201pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T_{p}S^{{\kern-9.36029pt{\mu}\kern 7.14026pt}}_{{\kern-13.99991pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.14026pt}}}
,
g\in\mathcal{T}_{\mu(p)}
;

(ii)

\theta_{p}((\mathbscr{a}_{*})_{p}(\alpha))=\alpha
, for all
\alpha\in T_{\mu(p)}^{*}M
and
p\in S
.

We call such a connection
1
-form
Lagrangian
if
\ker(\theta)_{p}\subset(T_{p}S,\omega_{p})
is Lagrangian for every
p\in\mathring{S}:=\mu^{-1}(\mathring{\Delta})
.

Remark 4.27
.

In the case of torus actions the notion of elliptic connection already appears in
[
12
]
.

Example 4.28
.

An elliptic Lagrangian connection for the standard toric local model
\mu_{k,n}:(S_{k,n},\omega_{k,n})\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
is given by

\theta(r_{i}\partial_{r_{i}})=0,\quad\theta(\partial_{\phi_{i}})=\mathrm{d}x^{i},\quad\theta(\partial_{x^{i}})=0,\quad\theta(\partial_{\theta_{i}})=\mathrm{d}x^{i+k}.

Note that this has zero radial residue along all open facets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{k}
.

Proposition 4.29
.

Every toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-space admits an elliptic Lagrangian connection
1
-form with zero radial residue along all open facets.

Proof.

In view of the above example such connection
1
-forms exist locally. A global such
1
-form can be constructed from these using a partition of unity for the manifold with corners
\Delta:=\mu(S)
, as in the proof of Proposition
3.13
.
∎

Next, we turn to the curvature of such connection
1
-forms. Let
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
be a toric Hamiltonian
\mathcal{T}
-space. There is a flat
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}}
-connection on the bundle
\mu^{*}T^{*}M
obtained by first pulling-back the canonical flat connection
\nabla
on
T^{*}M
along
\mu
to a
TS
-connection on
\mu^{*}T^{*}M
and then composing with the anchor of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}}
. This gives a differential

\mathrm{d}^{\nabla}:\Omega^{\bullet}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern
