g\in\Gamma^{\infty}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-17.53314pt{b}\kern 15.31912pt}}_{{\kern-22.15953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.31912pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-17.53314pt{b}\kern 15.31912pt}}_{{\kern-22.15953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.31912pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-11.49052pt{b}\kern 9.87733pt}}_{{\kern-14.79509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 9.87733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-9.31128pt{b}\kern 7.69809pt}}_{{\kern-12.61584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.69809pt}}}\otimes T^{*}\Delta),

(7.1)

we let
g^{\flat}:\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-9.24052pt{b}\kern 7.62733pt}}_{{\kern-12.54509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.62733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-7.06128pt{b}\kern 5.44809pt}}_{{\kern-10.36584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.44809pt}}}\to T^{*}\Delta
denote the induced vector bundle map. We also denote by

\widehat{g}\in\Gamma^{\infty}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-17.53314pt{b}\kern 15.31912pt}}_{{\kern-22.15953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.31912pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-17.53314pt{b}\kern 15.31912pt}}_{{\kern-22.15953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.31912pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-11.49052pt{b}\kern 9.87733pt}}_{{\kern-14.79509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 9.87733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-9.31128pt{b}\kern 7.69809pt}}_{{\kern-12.61584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.69809pt}}}\otimes\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-17.53314pt{b}\kern 15.31912pt}}_{{\kern-22.15953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.31912pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-17.53314pt{b}\kern 15.31912pt}}_{{\kern-22.15953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.31912pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-11.49052pt{b}\kern 9.87733pt}}_{{\kern-14.79509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 9.87733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-9.31128pt{b}\kern 7.69809pt}}_{{\kern-12.61584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.69809pt}}})

the section induced by
g
via the anchor map of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-9.24052pt{b}\kern 7.62733pt}}_{{\kern-12.54509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.62733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-7.06128pt{b}\kern 5.44809pt}}_{{\kern-10.36584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.44809pt}}}
, so that
\widehat{g}^{\flat}=\rho^{*}\circ g^{\flat}
.

Definition 7.4
.

A smooth section
g
of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-17.53314pt{b}\kern 15.31912pt}}_{{\kern-22.15953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.31912pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-17.53314pt{b}\kern 15.31912pt}}_{{\kern-22.15953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.31912pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-11.49052pt{b}\kern 9.87733pt}}_{{\kern-14.79509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 9.87733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-9.31128pt{b}\kern 7.69809pt}}_{{\kern-12.61584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.69809pt}}}\otimes T^{*}\Delta
will be called:

(i)

non-degenerate
if
g^{\flat}_{x}
is an isomorphism for each
x\in\Delta
;

(ii)

symmetric
if
\widehat{g}_{x}
is symmetric for all
x\in\Delta
(equivalently, for all
x\in\mathring{\Delta}
);

(iii)

positive semi-definite
if
\widehat{g}_{x}
is positive semi-definite for all
x\in\Delta
(equivalently, for all
x\in\mathring{\Delta}
).

We will call
g
a
hybrid
b
-metric
on
\Delta
if it satisfies all these three properties.

A hybrid
b
-metric on
\Delta
can be thought of as a smooth Riemannian metric on
\mathring{\Delta}
with specified singularities at the boundary. In local coordinates, these singularities are of the same type as those of the singular metrics in
[
1
]
. Assume
\Delta
is an open in
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{k}
. A smooth section
g
of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-17.53314pt{b}\kern 15.31912pt}}_{{\kern-22.15953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.31912pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-17.53314pt{b}\kern 15.31912pt}}_{{\kern-22.15953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.31912pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-11.49052pt{b}\kern 9.87733pt}}_{{\kern-14.79509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 9.87733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T^{*}\Delta^{{\kern-9.31128pt{b}\kern 7.69809pt}}_{{\kern-12.61584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.69809pt}}}\otimes T^{*}\Delta
is symmetric if and only if it is of the form

g=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{1}{x^{i}}g_{ii}\mathrm{d}x^{i}\otimes\mathrm{d}x^{i}+\sum_{(i,j)\in I_{k}}g_{ij}\mathrm{d}x^{i}\otimes\mathrm{d}x^{j},

(7.2)

for some
g_{ij}\in C^{\infty}(\Delta)
such that
g_{ij}=g_{ji}
for all
i,j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, where we denote

I_{k}:=\{(i,j)\in\{1,\dots,n\}^{2}\mid i\neq j\text{ or }i=j>k\}.

If
g
is symmetric, then

•

positive semi-definiteness of
g
means that for each
x\in\mathring{\Delta}
the matrix
\mathring{g}(x)
given by

\mathring{g}(x)_{ij}:=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{x^{i}}g_{ii}(x)\quad&\text{ if }i=j\leq k,\\
g_{ij}(x)\quad&\text{ if }(i,j)\in I_{k},\end{cases}

(7.3)

is positive semi-definite.

•

non-degeneracy of
g
at a point in
\Delta
means that

(x^{1}\cdot\dots\cdot x^{k})\cdot\det(\mathring{g})\in C^{\infty}(\Delta)

(7.4)

is non-zero at that point.

Therefore, if
g
is symmetric and positive semi-definite, then it is non-degenerate if and only if (
7.4
) is strictly positive on all of
\Delta
. The latter condition is as in
[
1
]
.

Assume now that
\Delta
is an affine manifold with corners, i.e.,
\Delta
has an atlas for which the transition functions are restrictions of affine transformations. An
Hessian hybrid
b
-metric

g
on
\Delta
is a hybrid
b
-metric satisfying

\mathrm{d}^{\nabla}g^{\flat}=0,

where
\nabla
is the associated flat
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-9.24052pt{b}\kern 7.62733pt}}_{{\kern-12.54509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.62733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-7.06128pt{b}\kern 5.44809pt}}_{{\kern-10.36584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.44809pt}}}
-connection on
T^{*}\Delta
. Such a
b
-metric
g
admits local potentials around points in the boundary of
\Delta
which are of a very particular form.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
\Delta
is an affine manifold with corners and
g
a Hessian hybrid
b
-metric on
\Delta
. Around a point
x\in\partial\Delta
of depth
k
there exists a chart
(U,x^{i})
centered at
x
for which

g=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial^{2}\phi}{\partial x^{i}\partial x^{j}}\mathrm{d}x^{i}\otimes\mathrm{d}x^{j}

where
\phi
is a smooth function on
\mathring{\Delta}\cap U
of the form

\phi=\sum_{i=1}^{k}c_{i}x^{i}\log(x^{i})+f,\quad c_{i}:=g_{ii}(0)\in\mathbb{R},\quad f\in C^{\infty}(U).

(7.5)

Proof.

Fix an affine chart for
\Delta
centered at
x
onto an open
[0,\delta[^{k}\times]-\delta,\delta[^{n-k}
. The fact that
g
is Hessian means that the coefficients (
7.3
) of the Riemannian metric on
\mathring{\Delta}
underlying
g
satisfy the usual set of differential equations

\frac{\partial\mathring{g}^{ij}}{\partial x^{l}}=\frac{\partial{\mathring{g}^{il}}}{\partial x^{j}},\quad 1\leq i,j,l\leq n.

(7.6)

In terms of the functions
g_{ij}\in C^{\infty}(U)
as in (
7.2
) this means that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{ii}}{\partial x^{j}}=x^{i}\frac{\partial g_{ij}}{\partial x^{i}}

\displaystyle\quad\text{if }i\leq k,\text{ }i\neq j,

(7.7)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{ij}}{\partial x^{l}}=\frac{\partial g_{il}}{\partial x^{j}}

\displaystyle\quad\text{if }(i,j),(i,l)\in I_{k}.

(7.8)

Since (
7.7
) vanishes if
x^{i}=0
, for
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
the function

x\mapsto g_{ii}(x^{1},\dots,x^{i-1},0,x^{i+1},\dots,x^{n})

is constant on
U
with value
c_{i}
. Since
g_{ii}(x)-c_{i}
vanishes if
x^{i}=0
,
g_{ii}-c_{i}
is of the form
x^{i}h_{i}
for a smooth function
h_{i}\in C^{\infty}(U)
. The equations (
7.7
) and (
7.8
) then express the fact that the
1
-forms
\alpha_{i}
on
U
given by

\alpha_{i}=\begin{cases}h_{i}\mathrm{d}x^{i}+\sum_{j\neq i}g_{ij}\mathrm{d}x^{j}\quad&\text{if }1\leq i\leq k,\\
\\
\sum_{j=1}^{n}g_{ij}\mathrm{d}x^{j},\quad&\text{if }k<i\leq n,\end{cases}

are closed. By the Poincaré lemma for manifolds with corners, each
\alpha_{i}
must in fact be exact. So, there are
\phi_{i}\in C^{\infty}(U)
such that
\mathrm{d}\phi_{i}=\alpha_{i}
. Applying the Poincaré lemma once more, this time to the closed
1
-form
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{i}\mathrm{d}x^{i}
on
U
, we find an
f\in C^{\infty}(U)
such that:

\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{i}\partial x^{j}}=\begin{cases}h_{i}\quad&\text{if }i=j<k,\\
\\
g_{ij}\quad&\text{if }(i,j)\in I_{k},\end{cases}

with
I_{k}
as in (
7.3
). For this choice of
f
, the function
\phi
defined by (
7.5
) is indeed a potential for
g
on
\mathring{\Delta}\cap U
.
∎

Next, we introduce the notion of residue appearing in Theorem
7.1
.

Definition 7.6
.

The
residue
of a hybrid
b
-metric
g
at an open facet of
\Delta
is the residue of the vector valued 1-form
g^{\flat}\in\Omega^{1}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-9.24052pt{b}\kern 7.62733pt}}_{{\kern-12.54509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.62733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-7.06128pt{b}\kern 5.44809pt}}_{{\kern-10.36584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.44809pt}}},T^{*}\Delta)
at the facet (see Definition
4.21
).

Example 7.7
.

Consider a Delzant polytope

\Delta:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{d}\{\ell_{i}\leq 0\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Then
\mathring{\Delta}
is contractible, so any Hessian hybrid
b
-metric on
\Delta
admits a global potential
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathring{\Delta})
. By applying successively Proposition
7.5
, one concludes that for a Delzant polytope a Hessian hybrid
b
-metric as in Theorem
7.1
(ii) has potential of the form

\phi(x)=-\tfrac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\ell_{i}(x)\log\ell_{i}(x)+f(x),

where
f
is smooth on
\Delta
(not just on
\mathring{\Delta}
). In view of this and (
7.4
), we recover the type of singular Hessian metrics on Delzant polytopes appearing in the works of Guillemin
[
24
]
and Abreu
[
1
]
.

Remark 7.8
.

In
[
1
]
Abreu also observed that the difference between any two of his singular hessian metrics on a Delzant polytope extends smoothly over the boundary. This holds more generally for any two hybrid
b
-metrics on a manifold with corners
\Delta
that have the same residues.

For the proof of Theorem
7.1
, it will be useful to also consider the notion dual to that of the singular metrics above. These will be smooth sections

\delta\in\Gamma^{\infty}(T\Delta\otimes\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-9.24052pt{b}\kern 7.62733pt}}_{{\kern-12.54509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.62733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-7.06128pt{b}\kern 5.44809pt}}_{{\kern-10.36584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.44809pt}}}).

Given such a section
\delta
, we denote by
\delta^{\sharp}:T^{*}\Delta\to\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-9.24052pt{b}\kern 7.62733pt}}_{{\kern-12.54509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.62733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-7.06128pt{b}\kern 5.44809pt}}_{{\kern-10.36584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.44809pt}}}
the induced vector bundle map. We also denote by

\widehat{\delta}\in\Gamma^{\infty}(T\Delta\otimes T\Delta)

the section induced by
\delta
via the anchor map of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-9.24052pt{b}\kern 7.62733pt}}_{{\kern-12.54509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.62733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-7.06128pt{b}\kern 5.44809pt}}_{{\kern-10.36584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.44809pt}}}
, so that
\widehat{\delta}^{\sharp}=\rho\circ\delta^{\sharp}
.

Definition 7.9
.

A smooth section
\delta
of
T\Delta\otimes\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-9.24052pt{b}\kern 7.62733pt}}_{{\kern-12.54509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.62733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-7.06128pt{b}\kern 5.44809pt}}_{{\kern-10.36584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.44809pt}}}
will be called:

(i)

non-degenerate
if
\delta^{\sharp}_{x}
is an isomorphism for each
x\in\Delta
;

(ii)

symmetric
if
\widehat{\delta}_{x}
is symmetric for all
x\in\Delta
(equivalently, for all
x\in\mathring{\Delta}
);

(iii)

positive semi-definite
if
\widehat{\delta}_{x}
is positive semi-definite for all
x\in\Delta
(equivalently, for all
x\in\mathring{\Delta}
).

We will call
\delta
a
hybrid
b^{*}
-metric
on
\Delta
if it satisfies all these three properties.

A hybrid
b^{*}
-metric has an underlying metric on
T^{*}\mathring{\Delta}
that extends smoothly to the tensor
\widehat{\delta}
on all of
\Delta
. Note, however, that
\widehat{\delta}
is degenerate at the boundary. To see how, suppose that
\Delta
is an open around the origin in
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{k}
. Then a smooth section
\delta
of
T\Delta\otimes\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-14.58315pt{b}\kern 12.36913pt}}_{{\kern-19.20953pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 12.36913pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-9.24052pt{b}\kern 7.62733pt}}_{{\kern-12.54509pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.62733pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T\Delta^{{\kern-7.06128pt{b}\kern 5.44809pt}}_{{\kern-10.36584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.44809pt}}}
is symmetric if and only if it is of the form

\displaystyle\delta=\sum_{i,j=1,j\neq i}^{k}x^{i}x^{j}\delta^{ij}{\partial}_{x^{i}}\otimes{\partial}_{x^{j}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{k}x^{i}\delta^{ii}{\partial}_{x^{i}}\otimes{\partial}_{x^{i}}+\sum_{i,j=k+1}^{n}\delta^{ij}{\partial}_{x^{i}}\otimes{\partial}_{x^{j}}

(7.9)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}x^{i}\delta^{ij}\left({\partial}_{x^{i}}\otimes{\partial}_{x^{j}}+{\partial}_{x^{j}}\otimes{\partial}_{x^{i}}\right),

for
\delta^{ij}\in C^{\infty}(\Delta)
such that
\delta^{ij}=\delta^{ji}
for all
1\leq i,j\leq n
. For positive semi-definiteness and non-degeracy, there are criteria analogous to those for hybrid
b
-metrics.

On a given manifold with corners, hybrid
b
-metrics are in bijective correspondence with hybrid
b^{*}
-metrics, in which a hybrid
b
-metric
g
and its corresponding hybrid
b^{*}
-metric
\delta
are related by the fact that
g^{\flat}
and
\delta^{\sharp}
are inverse to each other.

7.2.
Proof of Theorem
7.1

In this section we prove the two directions of the statement of Theorem
7.1
. We start by proving the following local result concerning invariant metrics in the local model discussed in Section
4.1
.

Lemma 7.10
.

Let
(S_{k,n},\omega_{k,n})
be a standard local model with the
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-action (
4.1
), and let
U
be an open in
\Delta:=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{k}
. Then:

(i)

A smooth
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant symmetric tensor:

G:\mu_{k,n}^{-1}(U\cap\mathring{\Delta})\to T^{*}S_{k,n}\otimes T^{*}S_{k,n}

extends smoothly to all of
\mu_{k,n}^{-1}(U)
if and only if it takes the following form

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left(g_{r_{j},r_{j}}(\mathrm{d}r_{j})^{2}+r_{j}^{3}g_{r_{j},\phi_{j}}\mathrm{d}r_{j}\mathrm{d}\phi_{j}+r_{j}^{2}g_{\phi_{j},\phi_{j}}(\mathrm{d}\phi_{j})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j,l=1,j\not=l}^{k}\left(r_{j}r_{l}g_{r_{j},r_{l}}\mathrm{d}r_{j}\mathrm{d}r_{l}+r_{j}r_{l}^{2}g_{r_{j},\phi_{l}}\mathrm{d}r_{j}\mathrm{d}\phi_{l}+r_{j}^{2}r_{l}^{2}g_{\phi_{j},\phi_{l}}\mathrm{d}\phi_{j}\mathrm{d}\phi_{l}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,l=1}^{k,n-k}\left(r_{j}g_{r_{j},x^{l}}\mathrm{d}r_{j}\mathrm{d}x^{l}+r_{j}g_{r_{j},\theta_{l}}\mathrm{d}r_{j}\mathrm{d}\theta_{l}+r_{j}^{2}g_{\phi_{j},x^{l}}\mathrm{d}\phi_{j}\mathrm{d}x^{l}+r_{j}^{2}g_{\phi_{j},\theta_{l}}\mathrm{d}\phi_{j}\mathrm{d}\theta_{l}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{j,l=1}^{n-k}\left(g_{\theta_{j},\theta_{l}}\mathrm{d}\theta_{j}\mathrm{d}\theta_{l}+g_{x^{j},\theta_{l}}\mathrm{d}x^{j}\mathrm{d}\theta_{l}+g_{x^{j},x^{l}}\mathrm{d}x^{j}\mathrm{d}x^{l}\right)

where the coefficients functions are
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant smooth functions on
\mu_{k,n}^{-1}(U)
and
g_{\phi_{j},\phi_{j}}=(2\pi)^{2}g_{r_{j},r_{j}}
over
U\cap\{x^{j}=0\}
.

(ii)

Dually, a smooth
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant symmetric tensor

G^{*}:\mu_{k,n}^{-1}(U\cap\mathring{\Delta})\to TS_{k,n}\otimes TS_{k,n}

extends smoothly to all of
\mu_{k,n}^{-1}(U)
if and only if its coefficients with respect to the coframe on
\mathring{S}

\mathrm{d}\phi_{1},\dots,\mathrm{d}\phi_{k},\frac{1}{r_{1}}\mathrm{d}r_{1},\dots,\frac{1}{r_{k}}\mathrm{d}r_{k},\mathrm{d}\theta_{1},\dots,\mathrm{d}\theta_{n-k},\mathrm{d}x^{1},\dots,\mathrm{d}x^{n-k}

are
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant smooth functions on
\mu_{k,n}^{-1}(U)
, with the exception of the following coefficients, which take the form

G^{*}\left(\frac{1}{r_{j}}\mathrm{d}r_{j},\frac{1}{r_{j}}\mathrm{d}r_{j}\right)=\frac{1}{r_{j}^{2}}g^{*}_{r_{j},r_{j}},\quad G^{*}\left(\mathrm{d}\phi_{j},\mathrm{d}\phi_{j}\right)=\frac{1}{r_{j}^{2}}g^{*}_{\phi_{j},\phi_{j}},

with
g^{*}_{r_{j},r_{j}}
and
g^{*}_{\phi_{j},\phi_{j}}

\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant smooth functions on
\mu_{k,n}^{-1}(U)
such that
g^{*}_{\phi_{j},\phi_{j}}=(2\pi)^{2}g^{*}_{r_{j},r_{j}}
over
U\cap\{x^{j}=0\}
.

Proof.

The backward implication in (i) readily follows by expressing the coefficients of
G
with respect to the frame:

\partial_{u_{1}},\partial_{v_{1}},\dots,\partial_{u_{k}},\partial_{v_{k}},\partial_{\theta_{1}},\dots,\partial_{\theta_{n-k}},\partial_{x^{1}},\dots,\partial_{x^{n-k}}

on
U
in terms of the coefficients with respect to the frame (
4.5
) on
\mathring{U}
. For the forward implication, suppose that
G
extends smoothly to all of
\mu^{-1}_{k,n}(U)
. By continuity this extension is
T
-invariant as well, hence so are its coefficient functions with respect to the frame (
4.5
). Applying Proposition
4.6
to these, it follows that the coefficients in the last 3 lines of the formula for
G
are of the desired form. Moreover, it follows that there are
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant smooth functions
g_{\phi_{j},\phi_{j}},g_{r_{j},r_{j}},\widetilde{g}_{\phi_{j},r_{j}}
on
\mu^{-1}_{k,n}(U)
such that:

\displaystyle G\left(\partial_{\phi_{j}},\partial_{\phi_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{j}^{2}g_{\phi_{j},\phi_{j}},

\displaystyle G\left(r_{j}\partial_{r_{j}},r_{j}\partial_{r_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{j}^{2}g_{r_{j},r_{j}},

\displaystyle G\left(\partial_{\phi_{j}},r_{j}\partial_{r_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{j}^{2}\widetilde{g}_{\phi_{j},r_{j}}.

Denote the smooth extension of
G
to
\mu^{-1}_{k,n}(U)
by
\widehat{G}
. Comparing the limits of the function
\widehat{G}(\partial_{u_{j}},\partial_{v_{j}})
along the two paths:

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\ni\varepsilon\mapsto(z_{1},\dots,z_{j-1},\varepsilon,z_{j+1},\dots,z_{k},t,x)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\ni\varepsilon\mapsto(z_{1},\dots,z_{j-1},i\varepsilon,z_{j+1},\dots,z_{k},t,x)

as
\varepsilon
tends to zero (for any given
j\in\{1,\dots,k\}
and
(z,t,x)\in U\cap\{z_{j}=0\}
) leads to the conclusion that
\widetilde{g}_{\phi_{j},r_{j}}
vanishes over
U\cap\{x^{j}=0\}
, so
\widetilde{g}_{\phi_{j},r_{j}}=r_{j}^{2}{g}_{\phi_{j},r_{j}}
. Computing the same limits for the function
\widehat{G}(\partial_{u_{j}},\partial_{u_{j}})
shows that
g_{\phi_{j},\phi_{j}}
and
(2\pi)^{2}\cdot g_{r_{j},r_{j}}
coincide over
U\cap\{x^{j}=0\}
, which completes the proof of (i). Part (ii) follows from the same type of arguments.
∎

7.2.1.
From invariant (Kähler) metrics to hybrid
b
-metrics

Let
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
be a toric
(\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda},\Omega_{\Lambda})
-space with Delzant subspace
\Delta:=\mu(S)
. Further, let
G

\omega

, the
\mathcal{T}

implies that there is a unique symmetric, positive semi-definite section
\widehat{\delta}\in\Gamma^{\infty}(T\Delta\otimes T\Delta)
making the following diagrams commute

(7.10)

The tensor
\widehat{\delta}
extends the metric on
T^{*}\mathring{\Delta}
dual to the unique Riemannian metric on

on
\mathring{\Delta}
making

a Riemannian submersion. For each locally defined

.
\textstyle{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-14.15633pt{\mu}\kern 11.09273pt}}_{{\kern-20.6518pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 11.09273pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-9.0511pt{\mu}\kern 6.83107pt}}_{{\kern-13.69072pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.83107pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}TS^{{\kern-7.09935pt{\mu}\kern 4.87932pt}}_{{\kern-11.73897pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.87932pt}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mu^{*}(T^{*}\Delta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(G^{\flat})^{-1}\circ(\mathrm{d}\mu)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{TS}

(7.11)

From this and the commutativity of the right-hand square in (
7.10
), it follows that
\widehat{\delta}^{\sharp}
takes

-forms on

to

-vector fields. Therefore,

(7.13)

will be a section of

It remains to prove that:

(i)

is non-degenerate at each

;

Let us fix

and standard toric coordinates

centered at

(see Section

). In these coordinates,
the smooth section

of

takes the form (

), so we can write the map

as a matrix of the form

\hline\cr 0&\hskip-5.0pt\vline\hskip-5.0pt&D_{n-k}\end{pmatrix},

(7.14)

with respect to standard bases of

and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T_{x}\Delta^{{\kern-17.88362pt{b}\kern 15.6696pt}}_{{\kern-22.51001pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.6696pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T_{x}\Delta^{{\kern-17.88362pt{b}\kern 15.6696pt}}_{{\kern-22.51001pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.6696pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T_{x}\Delta^{{\kern-11.74086pt{b}\kern 10.12767pt}}_{{\kern-15.04543pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 10.12767pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{b}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}T_{x}\Delta^{{\kern-9.56161pt{b}\kern 7.94843pt}}_{{\kern-12.86618pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.94843pt}}}
for
\Delta=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{k}
, where
D_{k}
is the diagonal
k
by
k
matrix with
i^{\textrm{th}}
diagonal entry
\delta^{ii}(x)
,
D_{n-k}
is the
n-k
by
n-k
matrix with
(i,j)
-entry
\delta^{k+i,k+j}(x)
, and similarly for
D_{n-k,k}
. Since

\partial_{\phi_{1}},\dots,\partial_{\phi_{k}},\partial_{\theta_{1}},\dots,\partial_{\theta_{n-k}},

are the vector fields associated to standard coframe on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
by the infinitesimal action
\mathbscr{a}
, it follows from part (i) of Lemma
7.10
and the left diagram in (
7.10
) that

\delta^{ij}=\begin{cases}g_{\phi_{i},\phi_{j}}\quad&\text{ if }i,j\leq k,\\
g_{\phi_{i},\theta_{j-k}}\quad&\text{ if }i\leq k,\text{ }j\geq k+1,\\
g_{\phi_{j},\theta_{i-k}}\quad&\text{ if }i\geq k+1,\text{ }j\leq k,\\
g_{\theta_{i-k},\theta_{j-k}}\quad&\text{ if }i\geq k+1,\text{ }j\geq k+1.\end{cases}

(7.15)

From this and the fact that
G
is positive definite, it follows that the functions
\delta^{ii}
are strictly positive on
\Delta
and the matrix (
7.14
) is invertible. This proves item (i).

For items (ii) and (iii) we can further assume that the depth of
x
is
1
, i.e.,
k=1
. We are left to show that
\delta^{11}(x)=4\pi
. Since

2r\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}x^{1},\dots,\mathrm{d}x^{n-1},

are the
1
-forms obtained by pulling back the standard coframe on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
along
\mu
, it follows from part (ii) of Lemma
7.10
and the right diagram in (
7.10
) that

\delta^{ij}=\begin{cases}4\,g^{*}_{r,r},\\
2\,g^{*}_{r,x^{j-1}}\quad&\text{ if }\text{ }j\geq 2,\\
2\,g^{*}_{r,x^{i-1}}\quad&\text{ if }i\geq 2,\\
g^{*}_{x^{i-1},x^{j-1}}\quad&\text{ if }i\geq 2,\text{ }j\geq 2.\end{cases}

(7.16)

By considering the coefficient functions of
g
with respect to the frame

\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\phi},\partial_{r},\partial_{\theta_{1}},\dots,\partial_{\theta_{n-1}},\partial_{x^{1}},\dots,\partial_{x^{n-1}}

over
\mathring{S}
and those of
g^{*}
with respect to the dual coframe

r\mathrm{d}\phi,\mathrm{d}r,\mathrm{d}\theta_{1},\dots,\mathrm{d}\theta_{n-1},\mathrm{d}x^{1},\dots,\mathrm{d}x^{n-1}

over
\mathring{S}
, we obtain mutually inverse matrices of functions that converge as
z
tends to zero. Comparing the two limits as
z
tends to zero leads to the conclusion that

g^{*}_{r,r}(x)\,g_{r,r}(x)=1.

Combined with the fact that
g_{\phi,\phi}(x)=(2\pi)^{2}\,g_{r,r}(x)
by Lemma
7.10
, and with (
7.15
) and (
7.16
), it follows that
\delta^{11}(x)=4\pi
.

To prove item (iii), we ought to show that, for any
b
-vector field
\nu
that is Euler-like with respect to a open facet
F
, the lift of
2\,\nu
along (
7.13
) is Euler-like with respect to
S_{F}
. In view of Remark
4.20
, we can assume that
\nu=x^{1}\partial_{x^{1}}
. The lift of the
b
-vector field
2x^{1}\partial_{x^{1}}
is of the form

\widetilde{2x^{1}\partial_{x^{1}}}=r\partial_{r}+f^{\phi}\partial_{\phi}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}f^{\theta}_{j}\partial_{\theta_{j}},

where
f^{\phi}
and
f^{\theta}_{j}
are
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant smooth functions on
\mu_{1,n}^{-1}(U)
. By Remark
4.24
, it is enough to show that
f^{\phi}(x)=f^{\theta}_{1}(x)=\cdots=f^{\theta}_{n-1}(x)=0
whenever
x^{1}=0
. Note that over
\mathring{S}
one has

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=g\left(2\widetilde{x^{1}\partial_{x^{1}}},\partial_{\theta_{l}}\right)=r^{2}\,g_{r,\theta_{l}}+g_{\phi,\theta_{l}}\,f^{\phi}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}g_{\theta_{j},\theta_{l}}\,f^{\theta}_{j},

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\,g\left(2\widetilde{x^{1}\partial_{x^{1}}},\partial_{\phi}\right)=r^{2}\,g_{\phi,r}+g_{\phi,\phi}\,f^{\phi}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}g_{\phi,\theta_{j}}\,f^{\theta}_{j},

where we first we used that
\ker(\mathrm{d}\mu)
and
\mathcal{L}
are orthogonal over
\mathring{S}
, and then we applied Lemma
7.10
. In view of (
7.15
), letting
z
tend to zero it follows that
(f^{\phi}(x),f_{1}^{\theta}(x),\dots,f_{n-1}^{\theta}(x))
belongs to the kernel of the invertible matrix (
7.14
) whenever
x^{1}=0
. So,
f^{\phi}(x)=f^{\theta}_{1}(x)=\dots=f^{\theta}_{n-1}(x)=0
if
x^{1}=0
.

This concludes the proof of one direction of Theorem
7.1
.

7.2.2.
From hybrid
b
-metrics to invariant (Kähler) metrics

Let
\mu:(S,\omega)\to M
be a toric
(\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda},\Omega_{\Lambda})
-space with Delzant subspace
\Delta:=\mu(S)
. Further, let
\theta
be an elliptic connection and let
g
be a hybrid
b
-metric on
\Delta
as in the statement of the theorem. We use
\delta
to denote the corresponding hybrid
b^{*}
-metric and we let
G
be the unique invariant
\omega
-compatible Kähler metric on
\mathring{S}
such that
\mu:(\mathring{S},G)\to(\mathring{\Delta},g)
is a Riemannian submersion and
\ker\theta|_{\mathring{S}}=(\ker\mathrm{d}\mu)^{\perp}|_{\mathring{S}}
. By the proof of Theorem
6.2
, this data satisfies (
7.10
) on
\mathring{S}
. We ought to show that
G
extends to a Riemannian metric on all of
S
. This extension is then automatically invariant and compatible with
\omega
, due to density of
\mathring{S}
.

First, we will show that
G
extends smoothly to
S
as a tensor. Since this is a local property, we can verify this in standard toric coordinates
(z,\theta,x)
by showing that the coefficients of
G
are of the form in part (i) of Lemma
7.10
. Because the left square in (
7.10
) commutes, the coefficients of
G
involving only

\partial_{\phi_{1}},\dots,\partial_{\phi_{k}},\partial_{\theta_{1}},\dots,\partial_{\theta_{n-k}}

are of this form, with the required
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant smooth functions on
\mu_{k,n}^{-1}(U)
given by the pull-backs of the corresponding coefficients
\delta^{ij}
of
\delta
, as in (
7.9
). For the coefficients of
G
involving
r_{i}\partial_{r_{i}}
, notice that the horizontal lift of
2x^{i}{\partial}_{x^{i}}
must be of the form

\widetilde{2x^{i}{\partial}_{x^{i}}}=r_{i}\partial_{r_{i}}+r_{i}^{2}\,\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{k}f^{\phi}_{i,j}\partial_{\phi_{j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-k}f_{i,j}^{\theta}\partial_{\theta_{j}}\Big),

(7.17)

where
f^{\phi}_{j}
and
f^{\theta}_{j}
are
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant smooth functions on
\mu_{k,n}^{-1}(U)
. Indeed, this follows from the condition in (ii) in Theorem
7.1
, Remarks
4.20
and
4.24
, and Proposition
4.6
. Since the horizontal lift is orthogonal to
\ker\mathrm{d}\mu
, it follows that the coefficients
G\left(r_{i}\partial_{r_{i}},\partial_{\phi_{l}}\right)
and
G\left(r_{i}\partial_{r_{i}},\partial_{\theta_{l}}\right)
are of the required form, and that

G\left(\widetilde{2x^{i}{\partial}_{x^{i}}},\widetilde{2x^{l}\partial_{x^{l}}}\right)=G\left(\widetilde{2x^{i}{\partial}_{x^{i}}},r_{l}\partial_{r_{l}}\right).

(7.18)

for
i,l\leq k
. Since
\mu
is a Riemannian submersion, it holds that:

G\left(\widetilde{2x^{i}{\partial}_{x^{i}}},\widetilde{2x^{l}\partial_{x^{l}}}\right)=\begin{cases}r_{i}^{2}r_{l}^{2}\mu^{*}(g_{il})&\text{ if }i\neq l,\\
r_{i}^{2}\mu^{*}(g_{ii})&\text{ if }i=l,\end{cases}

with
g_{il}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U)
as in (
7.2
). So, it follows from (
7.18
) that the coefficients
G\left(r_{i}\partial_{r_{i}},r_{l}\partial_{r_{l}}\right)
are also of the required form, and that

g_{\phi_{i},\phi_{i}}=\mu_{k,n}^{*}(\delta^{ii})\quad\text{and}\quad g_{r_{i},r_{i}}=\mu^{*}_{k,n}(g_{ii})\quad\text{over}\quad x^{i}=0.

Since
g^{\flat}
and
\delta^{\sharp}
are inverse to each other, it holds that
g_{ii}(x)\delta^{ii}(x)=1
when
x^{i}=0
. By the assumption on the residue of
g
, one has that
g_{ii}(x)=\frac{1}{4\pi}
when
x^{i}=0
. Hence, it follows that

g_{\phi_{i},\phi_{i}}=(2\pi)^{2}g_{r_{i},r_{i}}\quad\text{over}\quad x^{i}=0.

By similar arguments, the other coefficients are of the required form as well. So, the tensor
G
indeed extends smoothly to all of
S
. This extension is symmetric and positive semi-definite, since it is so on the dense subset
\mathring{S}
. So, to conclude that it is a Riemannian metric on
S
, it suffices to show that the dual metric
G^{*}
on
\mathring{S}
extends smoothly, as a tensor, to all of
S
. This follows from an argument along the same lines as that for
G
, by instead using part (ii) of Lemma
7.10
and the coframe over
\mathring{S}\cap\mu^{-1}(U)
dual to the frame:

\widetilde{2x^{1}\partial_{x^{1}}},\dots,\widetilde{2x^{k}\partial_{x^{k}}},\widetilde{\partial_{x^{k+1}}},\dots,\widetilde{\partial_{x^{n}}},\partial_{\phi_{1}},\dots,\partial_{\phi_{k}},\partial_{\theta_{1}},\dots,\partial_{\theta_{n-k}}.

This concludes the proof of Theorem
7.1
.

7.3.
Examples

Example 7.11
.

Consider the integral affine cylinder
(M,\Lambda)=(\mathbb{S}^{1}\times\mathbb{R},\mathbb{Z}\mathrm{d}x\oplus\mathbb{Z}\mathrm{d}h)
and the Delzant subspace
\Delta=\mathbb{S}^{1}\times[-1,1]
as in Example
4.35
. Further, consider the corresponding canonical toric
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}
-space
\mu:(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2},\omega)\to\mathbb{S}^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
described in Example
2.20
. As in Example
6.10
, there are Hessian hybrid
b
-metrics of the form

g=\left(\frac{1}{4\pi(1-h^{2})}+f(h)\right)\mathrm{d}h^{2}+c(x)\,\mathrm{d}x^{2},

where now the singular term is so that the residue at both facets is
\tfrac{1}{4\pi}
. By a computation as in Example
6.10
, we find that the extremal condition holds if and only if
f=0
and
c(x)
is constant. Using the flat Lagrangian elliptic connection
D_{a,b}
of Example
4.35
, Theorem
7.1
gives the family of
\omega
-compatible extremal Kähler metrics

G=\left(\frac{1}{\tau(h)}+b^{2}\tau(h)\right)\mathrm{d}h^{2}+\tau(h)\mathrm{d}\phi(\mathrm{d}\phi-2b\mathrm{d}h)+g_{\mathbb{T}^{2}},

(7.19)

where
\tau(h)=4\pi(1-h^{2})
and
g_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}
is the flat metric on the torus given by

g_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}=\frac{1}{c}\big((a^{2}+c^{2})\mathrm{d}x^{2}-2a\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y+\mathrm{d}y^{2}\big).

Note that when
a=b=0
and
c=1
we recover the product of the round metric on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
with the standard flat metric on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
.

Example 7.12
.

Let
(M,\Lambda)=(\mathbb{S}^{1}\times]-2,\infty[,\mathbb{Z}((h+2)\mathrm{d}x+x\mathrm{d}h)\oplus\mathbb{Z}\mathrm{d}h)
be the non-standard integral affine cylinder and consider the Delzant subspace
\Delta=\mathbb{S}^{1}\times[-1,1]
(see Example
4.36
). Further, we consider the corresponding canonical toric
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}
-space
\mu:(\mathbb{T}^{2}\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.95134pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\cr$\times$\cr}}}\mathbb{S}^{2},\omega)\to\mathbb{S}^{1}\times]-2,\infty[
, which is described in Example
2.21
. Similar to Example
6.11
, there are Hessian hybrid
b
-metrics of the form

g=\left(\frac{1}{4\pi(1-h^{2})}+f(h)\right)\mathrm{d}h^{2}+(h+2)c\,\mathrm{d}x^{2},

with
c>0
a constant and
f\in C^{\infty}(]-2,\infty[)
. Let us define a function
\tau(h)
by

\frac{1}{\tau(h)}:=\frac{1}{4\pi(1-h^{2})}+f(h).

One finds the boundary conditions

\tau(\pm 1)=0,\quad\tau^{\prime}(\pm 1)=\mp 8\pi.

Applying formula (
6.8
), one finds that

S_{G}=-\frac{1}{2}\tau^{\prime\prime}(h)-\frac{\tau^{\prime}(h)}{h+2}.

Proceeding as in Example
6.11
, taking into account the boundary conditions, one finds that
S_{G}
is an affine function if and only if

\tau(h)=-\frac{4\pi}{11}\left(2(h+2)^{3}-5(h+2)^{2}-15+\frac{18}{h+2}\right).

This gives the affine function

S_{G}=\frac{4\pi}{11}(12h+9).

The corresponding hybrid Hessian
b
-metric takes the form

g=\left(\frac{1}{4\pi(1-h^{2})}+\frac{1}{2\pi(2h^{2}+11h+20)}\right)\mathrm{d}h^{2}+(h+2)c\,\mathrm{d}x^{2}.

We can now use the family of elliptic Lagrangian connections
D_{a,b}
on the
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}
-space
\mu:(\mathbb{T}^{2}\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.95134pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sim$}\cr$\times$\cr}}}\mathbb{S}^{2},\omega)\to\mathbb{S}^{1}\times]-2,\infty[
discussed in Example
4.36
to lift
g
to the family of
\omega
-compatible extremal Kähler metrics

G=\left(\frac{1}{\tau(h)}+b^{2}\tau(h)\right)\mathrm{d}h^{2}+\tau(h)\theta(\theta-2b\mathrm{d}h)+(h+2)g_{\mathbb{T}^{2}},

(7.20)

where
\theta:=(x\mathrm{d}y+\mathrm{d}\phi)
and
g_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}
is the flat metric on the torus given by

g_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}=\frac{1}{c}\big((a^{2}+c^{2})\mathrm{d}x^{2}-2a\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y+\mathrm{d}y^{2}\big).

Notice the similarity between formulas (
7.19
) and (
7.20
). When
b=0
, the metric (
7.20
) appears as a special case of the metrics considered by Apostolov et al.
[
2
]
(see the formula in Theorem 3, loc. cit.). The precise relation between the approach followed here and the results in
[
2
]
will be discussed in a sequel to this paper.

7.4.
Invariant Kähler metrics via Kähler reduction

The Delzant-type construction from Section
5
(see Theorem
5.11
) can be used to construct invariant Kähler metrics via Kähler reduction, much like in
[
24
]
.

Let
(M,\Lambda)
be a connected integral affine manifold and
\Delta\subset M
a Delzant subspace of finite type. Assume that
M
admits a Hessian metric
g
and let
\widetilde{g}
be its lift to the universal covering space
(\widetilde{M},\widetilde{\Lambda})
. Let
G_{\widetilde{\Lambda}}
be the
\Omega_{\widetilde{\Lambda}}
-compatible invariant Kähler metric on
\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\Lambda}}
obtained by lifting
\widetilde{g}
via the canonical Lagrangian
connection.

Proposition 7.13
.

Let
(M,\Lambda,g)
be a connected integral affine Hessian manifold and let
\Delta
be a Delzant subspace of finite type. Consider the product metric
G_{\widetilde{\Lambda}}\times g_{\mathrm{st}}
on
\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\Lambda}}\times\mathbb{C}^{d}
. The Hessian hybrid
b
-metric
g_{\Delta}
on
\Delta
induced by the reduced Kähler metric on the symplectic quotient

((\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\Lambda}}\times\mathbb{C}^{d})\mathbin{/\mkern-6.0mu/}(\Gamma\ltimes\mathbb{T}^{d}),\,\omega_{\mathrm{red}})

takes the form

g_{\Delta}=g+\mathrm{Hess}_{\Lambda}(\phi),

where
\phi
is the smooth function on
\mathring{\Delta}
corresponding to the function on
\widetilde{M}
given by

\widetilde{\phi}=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\ell_{i}\log|\ell_{i}|.

(7.21)

The proof follows the usual Kähler reduction recipe: one restricts the metric to the zero level, obtaining a
(\Gamma\ltimes\mathbb{T}^{d})
-invariant metric, that then descends to the symplectic quotient, yielding the expression in the theorem. Notice that, although each term in (
7.21
) is a function on
\widetilde{M}
which may fail to descend to
\mathring{\Delta}
, the sum does descend to a smooth function, since the
\Gamma
-action permutes the primitive boundary defining functions.

Remark 7.14
.

For a Delzant polytope, Guillemin showed in
[
24
]
that, if one applies Kähler reduction to the standard Delzant construction, one obtains an invariant Kähler metric on the symplectic toric manifold which induces a Hessian metric on
\mathring{\Delta}
with potential given by (
7.21
). Our Delzant type construction is different from the standard one (see Remark
5.10
) and results in the presence of the original Hessian metric on
M
as an additional term. This is unavoidable since for a general Delzant subspace of finite type there may not exist a global potential. Moreover, the Hessian of (
7.21
) may not even define a metric in
\mathring{\Delta}
. This is the case, for instance, in Examples
7.11
and

7.12
.
Also, the Hessian hybrid
b
-metrics constructed in those examples show that
\Delta
may carry Hessian hybrid
b
-metrics with residues as in Theorem
7.1
which are not obtained by Kähler reduction as in Proposition
7.13
.

Corollary 7.15
.

Any finite-type Delzant subspace of a Hessian integral affine manifold admits a Hessian hybrid
b
-metric with residues as in Theorem
7.1
.
